# Content Atelier — Editorial Intelligence Audit

Whitney Hodges · Wedding Industry Ads · 14 October 2026

This is a knowledge extraction report. It contains evidence and proposed system rules, not newly written marketing content. It is complete for the documented extraction pass, **not an exhaustive audit of all historical conversations or every accessible file**.

**How to read the evidence.** Exact quotations preserve words and punctuation; whitespace and PDF wrapping are normalized. A directly read source can establish what was written, said or requested, but cannot by itself certify a performance claim, publication approval, current price or the accuracy of a transcript. User-endorsed references are distinguished from original user messages and unapproved generated synthesis. Unknown dates and missing draft portions remain unknown.

Findings use stable P IDs, evidence uses E IDs, sources use S IDs, rules use R IDs, decisions use D IDs, conflicts use C IDs and gaps use GAP IDs. The companion JSON contains these same records and their relationships.

Dates follow the source’s labeling. A conversation start date is not necessarily the feedback-turn date. A Library upload date is not a recording or publication date. Slack displayed clock labels are preserved rather than silently converted. The June 1–7, 2026 test-batch date was supplied by the vendor; exact dates of individual selected calls remain unverified.

### Evidence precedence and integrity

For editorial choices, a later specific user instruction takes precedence over an older conflicting example or a broad reference endorsement. Original user feedback and clearly scoped approvals are strongest. User-endorsed current references are valid guidance but do not mean every suggested sentence was approved verbatim. Sent copy proves communication occurred; historical source compilations show examples. Generated handbooks and packs can suggest rules but cannot independently prove approval. Memory and retrieval summaries supply useful context with **MEMORY-ONLY / SOURCE NOT VERIFIED** labels where originals are missing.

The October 13 Slack instruction explicitly supplies the founder guide, completed WLF worksheet and Savannah strategy as Content Atelier inputs. This verifies their reference status. It does not approve every generated content pack, numerical threshold, historical claim or illustrative hook.

Credentials, access tokens, unrelated personal information and incidental financial material are excluded. Client names appearing below identify internal evidence; they do not imply permission to name the client publicly.

## Part B — Complete editorial intelligence profile

The central requirement is directly verified in Whitney’s original Content Atelier feedback: “We want this system to uncover content opportunities that demonstrate what we know, how we think, how we work with clients, and why our approach matters, not simply extract educational takeaways from conversations.” [Original October 12 feedback](https://weddingindustryads.slack.com/archives/C0BSM3Y6095/p1791862314565759?thread_ts=1791468432.366269&cid=C0BSM3Y6095).

This profile preserves each material distinction rather than collapsing it into a generic personality description. **CURRENT AND EXPLICIT** means explicitly stated in a current endorsed reference or directly documented source with no known superseding instruction; it does not certify that a dated business fact is still current today. **HISTORICAL** and **CONTEXT-DEPENDENT** prevent universal reuse. **INFERRED** is an interpretation. **UNVERIFIED** means the relevant original user source was not recovered. Confidence concerns evidence support, not predicted content performance.

For explicit-reference findings, “explicit instruction” includes an explicitly documented principle in an endorsed reference. The evidence-author/source fields distinguish it from a sentence Whitney personally typed. Findings with interpretation-only reasoning are marked accordingly.

### A. Marketing philosophy and core beliefs

#### P001 · Strategy precedes platform work

Advertising strategy starts with the intended buyer, meaningful differentiation and reasons to choose the business, before campaign setup.

**Context:** WIA caption about technically correct campaigns and foundational strategy.

**Evidence:**

* E001 — “And that part of the strategy gets built long before we ever hit publish.” [S013](https://weddingindustryads.slack.com/archives/C063XKRCJ0M/p1790886037934299?thread_ts=1790886037.934299&cid=C063XKRCJ0M); 2026-10-01; Whitney; Caption, message 1790886037.934299. Evidence type: original\_user\_message.
* E002 — “Ads don’t work in isolation” [S007](https://docs.google.com/document/d/17kqUaJIYVwA-rXciG3Tn9O-0pdOaZQy9/edit?usp=sharing&ouid=104440450174655659283&rtpof=true&sd=true); 2026-10-13 (reference endorsement); reference document; Completed WIA polarizing-opinion answers. Evidence type: user\_endorsed\_reference.

**Reasoning (supported\_by\_user\_source):** A technically correct setup cannot supply the buyer’s reason to choose the business.

**Strength and status:** explicit\_instruction; CURRENT AND EXPLICIT; evidence confidence high.

**Applicability:** global\_editorial. **Approval gates:** Angle, Brief, Draft.

#### P002 · Visibility must support decisions

Reach, clicks and traffic are useful steps; the desired business outcome is more of the right couples becoming clients.

**Context:** Full-funnel positioning caption and endorsed founder reference.

**Evidence:**

* E003 — “Turning more of the right couples into clients is.” [S013](https://weddingindustryads.slack.com/archives/C063XKRCJ0M/p1790887921759699?thread_ts=1790887921.759699&cid=C063XKRCJ0M); 2026-10-01; Whitney; Caption, message 1790887921.759699. Evidence type: original\_user\_message.
* E004 — “Marketing isn't about getting attention. It's about shaping decisions.” [S005](https://docs.google.com/document/d/1_bGFggqB1CH6QQhVIyrUaPLC6LOzI1_VCkuAv8Kug_Y/edit?usp=sharing); 2026-10-13 (reference endorsement); reference document; Core Beliefs. Evidence type: user\_endorsed\_reference.

**Reasoning (supported\_by\_user\_source):** The visibility argument needs an explanation of what happens after discovery.

**Strength and status:** explicit\_instruction; CURRENT AND EXPLICIT; evidence confidence high.

**Applicability:** global\_editorial. **Approval gates:** Angle, Brief, Draft.

Strong positioning, brand, content, reputation and website experience help paid visibility do more useful work.

**Context:** Established planner paused Instagram while ads continued.

**Evidence:**

* E005 — “Paid ads weren't making up for a lack of marketing. They were building on years of it.” [S013](https://weddingindustryads.slack.com/archives/C063XKRCJ0M/p1786651769475799?thread_ts=1786651769.475799&cid=C063XKRCJ0M); 2026-08-13; Whitney; Capacity carousel, message 1786651769.475799. Evidence type: original\_user\_message.
* E006 — “Ads amplify businesses. They don't fix them.” [S005](https://docs.google.com/document/d/1_bGFggqB1CH6QQhVIyrUaPLC6LOzI1_VCkuAv8Kug_Y/edit?usp=sharing); 2026-10-13 (reference endorsement); reference document; Core Beliefs. Evidence type: user\_endorsed\_reference.

**Reasoning (supported\_by\_user\_source):** The strategic lesson is continuity supported by earlier marketing investment.

**Strength and status:** explicit\_instruction; CURRENT AND EXPLICIT; evidence confidence high.

**Applicability:** global\_editorial. **Approval gates:** Angle, Brief, Draft.

**Boundary:** Do not turn this into a ban on advertising for new businesses; the worksheet documents a successful new-planner exception.

### B. Audience understanding

The completed worksheet documents an avatar of primarily women (80% women/20% men), typically ages 28–45, usually established owner/operators in growth or scaling. It includes venues, planners, photographers, videographers, florists, DJs, caterers, rental companies, bridal boutiques and luxury event professionals. Venues and planners are called especially suitable for ELEVATE. It emphasizes US markets and US-based destination work, with examples in Texas, California, Pennsylvania, New York, New Jersey and Tennessee. **These are the worksheet’s qualitative avatar estimates and experience notes, not audited population statistics or eligibility rules.** The same source preserves a successful new-business exception, and original Canadian prospect correspondence prevents a US-only interpretation. [Completed worksheet](https://docs.google.com/document/d/17kqUaJIYVwA-rXciG3Tn9O-0pdOaZQy9/edit?usp=sharing&ouid=104440450174655659283&rtpof=true&sd=true).

#### P019 · Address established owners as capable business people

WIA’s central audience often has a strong business, brand and marketing history; speak without implying ignorance.

**Context:** WIA’s completed target-audience worksheet.

**Evidence:**

* E032 — “Our ideal client is usually already established in the wedding industry.” [S007](https://docs.google.com/document/d/17kqUaJIYVwA-rXciG3Tn9O-0pdOaZQy9/edit?usp=sharing&ouid=104440450174655659283&rtpof=true&sd=true); 2026-10-13 (reference endorsement); reference document; Completed client story. Evidence type: user\_endorsed\_reference.
* E033 — “They trust that we’ll guide them clearly without making them feel stupid or behind.” [S007](https://docs.google.com/document/d/17kqUaJIYVwA-rXciG3Tn9O-0pdOaZQy9/edit?usp=sharing&ouid=104440450174655659283&rtpof=true&sd=true); 2026-10-13 (reference endorsement); reference document; Completed client story. Evidence type: user\_endorsed\_reference.

**Reasoning (user\_explained):** These readers may understand their differentiators yet lack a trusted advertising system.

**Strength and status:** explicit\_instruction; CURRENT AND EXPLICIT; evidence confidence high.

**Applicability:** global\_editorial. **Approval gates:** Angle, Brief, Draft.

#### P020 · Do not assume every reader has an inquiry crisis

Some clients want stability, quality, control or capacity even when already booked.

**Context:** Audience segmentation.

**Evidence:**

* E034 — “Others are fully booked but want more control, more stability, higher-quality inquiries, or the ability to scale” [S007](https://docs.google.com/document/d/17kqUaJIYVwA-rXciG3Tn9O-0pdOaZQy9/edit?usp=sharing&ouid=104440450174655659283&rtpof=true&sd=true); 2026-10-13 (reference endorsement); reference document; Completed client story. Evidence type: user\_endorsed\_reference.

### E. Content structure and creative direction

#### P065 · Use Savannah’s five-part content framework

Hook → Problems/Desires/Symptoms → Main Point → Transformation → CTA is the current endorsed sequence.

**Context:** Current strategy and founder reference agree on the structure.

**Evidence:**

* E092 — “Every piece of content should follow: Hook (Content idea / opening statement) Problems, Desires, Symptoms” [S006](https://docs.google.com/document/d/1JzN1sMvxnLFT0u3unAsQGt6J7tG0m3ja6E7koxqaYBc/edit?usp=sharing); 2026-10-13 (reference endorsement); reference document; Content Framework. Evidence type: user\_endorsed\_reference.

**Reasoning (documented\_reference\_reasoning):** The framework guides progression; it does not require visible labels or identical sentence templates.

**Strength and status:** explicit\_instruction; CURRENT AND EXPLICIT; evidence confidence high.

**Applicability:** finished\_WIA\_marketing\_content. **Approval gates:** Brief, Draft.

#### P066 · Transformation can be changed understanding

A lightbulb moment can fulfill the transformation step when the source does not contain measured outcomes.

**Context:** Thought-shift and process content.

**Evidence:**

* E093 — “Focuses on changed thinking as much as changed results.” [S005](https://docs.google.com/document/d/1_bGFggqB1CH6QQhVIyrUaPLC6LOzI1_VCkuAv8Kug_Y/edit?usp=sharing); 2026-10-13 (reference endorsement); reference document; Signature Content Structure. Evidence type: user\_endorsed\_reference.

**Reasoning (documented\_reference\_reasoning):** Transformation need not be a fabricated revenue gain.

**Strength and status:** explicit\_instruction; CURRENT AND EXPLICIT; evidence confidence high.

**Applicability:** global\_editorial. **Approval gates:** Brief, Draft.

Evidence type: user\_endorsed\_reference.

**Reasoning (documented\_reference\_reasoning):** A sales invitation need not be the purpose of every educational or trust-building piece.

Evidence type: original\_business\_correspondence.

### H. Subtle preferences and recurring patterns

#### P102 · Accuracy precedes persuasive polish

Repeated decisions favor meaningful differentiation without sacrificing factual accuracy.

**Context:** Messaging, results and product scope decisions.

**Evidence:**

* E154 — “The goal is compelling differentiation without sacrificing accuracy.” [S004](https://docs.google.com/document/d/1_LRWdO-rQ2ez9J0IWffrx9gt0L6j2nNlmH01iHxYuag/edit?usp=sharing); 2026-10-12; reference document; Alternative angle 2. Evidence type: user\_endorsed\_reference.
* E155 — “I wouldn't want to cherry-pick individual client results” [S011](https://mail.google.com/mail/u/?authuser=hello@weddingindustryads.com#all/1a0ce5a002673a12); 2026-09-23; Whitney; Sent due-diligence response. Evidence type: original\_business\_correspondence.

**Reasoning (analyst\_interpretation):** This interpretation is supported across different tasks; it is more specific than authentic content.

**Strength and status:** repeated\_demonstrated\_preference; CURRENT AND EXPLICIT; evidence confidence moderate.

**Applicability:** global\_editorial. **Approval gates:** Angle, Brief, Draft.

#### P103 · Scope-aware warmth is a recurring communication pattern

Whitney can validate a question, explain a boundary and offer the appropriate next step without sounding cold.

**Context:** Branding and migration replies.

**Evidence:**

* E156 — “That said, once we get into onboarding, I'm absolutely happy to give you some high-level feedback” [S012](https://mail.google.com/mail/u/?authuser=hello@weddingindustryads.com#all/1a10d31d2c193766); 2026-10-05; Whitney; Sent scope explanation. Evidence type: original\_business\_correspondence.
* E157 — “I'm glad you asked because I think there are a couple of important distinctions” [S010](https://mail.google.com/mail/u/?authuser=hello@weddingindustryads.com#all/1a0ee674fa254a7f); 2026-09-29; Whitney; September 29 sent response. Evidence type: original\_business\_correspondence.

**Reasoning (analyst\_interpretation):** Two different correspondence contexts show the same sequence of acknowledgment, boundary and useful next step.

**Strength and status:** repeated\_demonstrated\_preference; CURRENT AND EXPLICIT; evidence confidence moderate.

**Applicability:** client\_and\_prospect\_correspondence. **Approval gates:** Angle, Brief, Draft.

#### P104 · Simple next steps reduce friction

A one-question reply or clearly linked next action can be more useful than a pile of requests.

**Context:** Research reply request and onboarding sequencing.

**Evidence:**

* E158 — “Please hit reply and type A, B, C, or D, whichever answer feels most aligned for you.” [S016](https://weddingindustryads.slack.com/archives/C0AP1P4DE1L/p1781721903784389?thread_ts=1781539930.511789&cid=C0AP1P4DE1L); 2026-06-17; Whitney; Message 1781721903.784389. Evidence type: original\_user\_message.
* E159 — “I want her to get her onboarding calls booked before I ask anything else of her.” [S016](https://weddingindustryads.slack.com/archives/D096WQEB16H/p1787159093513499?thread_ts=1787155664.727189&cid=D096WQEB16H); 2026-08-19; Whitney; Message 1787159093.513499. Evidence type: original\_user\_message.

**Reasoning (analyst\_interpretation):** Several contexts support reducing cognitive load, but this is not a permanent ban on multi-part useful forms.

**Strength and status:** repeated\_demonstrated\_preference; CURRENT AND EXPLICIT; evidence confidence moderate.

**Applicability:** correspondence\_and\_CTAs. **Approval gates:** Angle, Brief, Draft.

#### P105 · The desired shift is often a clearer way of thinking

The preferred impression is a familiar issue made newly understandable, not novelty for its own sake.

**Context:** Founder reference and original Content Atelier correction.

**Evidence:**

* E160 — “I hadn't looked at it that way before.” [S005](https://docs.google.com/document/d/1_bGFggqB1CH6QQhVIyrUaPLC6LOzI1_VCkuAv8Kug_Y/edit?usp=sharing); 2026-10-13 (reference endorsement); reference document; AI Style Guide. Evidence type: user\_endorsed\_reference.
* E161 — “recognize the bigger picture” [S002](https://weddingindustryads.slack.com/archives/C0BSM3Y6095/p1791862314565759?thread_ts=1791468432.366269&cid=C0BSM3Y6095); 2026-10-12; Whitney; Original critique. Evidence type: original\_user\_message.

**Reasoning (analyst\_interpretation):** Both sources center interpretation and strategic thinking.

**Strength and status:** repeated\_demonstrated\_preference; CURRENT AND EXPLICIT; evidence confidence moderate.

**Applicability:** global\_editorial. **Approval gates:** Angle, Brief, Draft.

#### P106 · Evidence can support a confident recommendation without proving a result

Process content can be persuasive while explicitly staying within a recommendation, proposal or buyer insight.

**Context:** Preferred Content Atelier directions.

**Evidence:**

* E162 — “Do not imply WIA independently originated the revised phrase or that a change improved conversions.” [S004](https://docs.google.com/document/d/1_LRWdO-rQ2ez9J0IWffrx9gt0L6j2nNlmH01iHxYuag/edit?usp=sharing); 2026-10-12; reference document; Alternative angle 2 guardrail. Evidence type: user\_endorsed\_reference.

**Reasoning (analyst\_interpretation):** Confidence in the reasoning and certainty about measured outcomes are separate.

**Strength and status:** repeated\_demonstrated\_preference; CURRENT AND EXPLICIT; evidence confidence moderate.

**Applicability:** global\_editorial. **Approval gates:** Angle, Brief, Draft.

#### P107 · An approved reference is not approval of every artifact or sentence

Use endorsements, actual publication instructions and version-specific approvals as different evidence types.

**Context:** Guide endorsement and preferred-angle status.

**Evidence:**

* E164 — “Below are the resources I'd like the Content Atelier to take into account:” [S003](https://weddingindustryads.slack.com/archives/C0BSM3Y6095/p1791898651102679); 2026-10-13; Whitney; Original message. Evidence type: original\_user\_message.
* E165 — “These are proposed content directions—not final hooks, scripts, or claims of proven performance.” [S004](https://docs.google.com/document/d/1_LRWdO-rQ2ez9J0IWffrx9gt0L6j2nNlmH01iHxYuag/edit?usp=sharing); 2026-10-12; reference document; Feedback tab opening. Evidence type: user\_endorsed\_reference.

**Reasoning (analyst\_interpretation):** The source deliberately distinguishes a reference, a direction and finished copy.

**Strength and status:** repeated\_demonstrated\_preference; CURRENT AND EXPLICIT; evidence confidence moderate.

**Applicability:** global\_editorial. **Approval gates:** Angle, Brief, Draft.

### Vocabulary and recurring expression references

These lists preserve actual reference language. They are not a mandate to sprinkle favorite nouns or catchphrases throughout every post. Client landing-page constructions have their own scope; an instruction to avoid “Imagine...” as formulaic copy does not oppose helping couples imagine an experience.

|List|Exact terms/phrases|Scope and source|
|-|-|-|
|LEX001|Strategy; Foundation; Momentum; Trust; Visibility; Perspective; Alignment; Systems; Buyer psychology; Predictable; Intentional; Confidence; Decision; Experience; Clarity|WIA founder voice; illustrative natural vocabulary, not forced keywords; [S005](#s005), Vocabulary / Natural language|
|LEX002|Hack; Secret; Explode; Crush it; Dominate; Game-changing; Insane; Magic; Guru; Skyrocket (unless directly quoting a result)|New WIA founder copy; source preserves direct-result quotation exception for Skyrocket; [S005](#s005), Vocabulary / Words to avoid|
|LEX003|"What's fascinating is..."; "I've been thinking about..."; "I had a conversation with one of our clients..."; "The interesting thing is..."; "But here's what stood out to me..."; "The bigger lesson is..."; "Whether that's intentional or not is almost beside the point..."; "That's what great marketing does."; "The goal isn't..."; "The real goal is..."|Illustrative phrases; do not repeat as mandatory catchphrases or override later exclusions; [S005](#s005), This Sounds Like Whitney|
|LEX004|"Here's the secret..."; "Nobody is talking about this..."; "This will change everything."; "You're leaving money on the table."; "Six figures in 90 days."; "If you're not doing this, you're already behind."; "Growth hack."; "The algorithm loves..."|New WIA founder copy; historical originals may contain these constructions; [S005](#s005), This Doesn't Sound Like Whitney|
|LEX005|Where dreams come true; Your perfect day starts here; Creating unforgettable memories; Bringing your vision to life; Your dream wedding awaits; More than just...; Whether you're X or Y...; From X to Y...; Imagine...; Picture this...; Your dream X awaits; Look no further; We've got you covered; At \[Business], we believe...|Client paid landing-page writing; client-specific voice and source context apply; [S021](#s021), Headline Principle and Copy Standards|

## Part D — Real editorial decisions

The first six cases contain the actual original proposals and the actual preferred-direction blocks from the current native review document. The general user feedback applies to all six; individual rating cells were blank in the retrieved text. Subsequent cases distinguish full exchanges, partial recovery, verified final correspondence and memory-only reconstructions. Missing text is never filled with a new invented draft.

### D001 · Angle 1: Give distinct wedding markets distinct landing page context → How we help wedding pros make their landing pages relevant to the couples they actually want to book

**Source/date:** [Open source](https://docs.google.com/document/d/1_LRWdO-rQ2ez9J0IWffrx9gt0L6j2nNlmH01iHxYuag/edit?usp=sharing); 2026-10-12. **Recovery status:** verified\_exchange.

**Original proposed content:**

Angle 1: Give distinct wedding markets distinct landing page context
Argument: When a wedding business serves regions with different visual expectations, a market-specific landing page can make the offer easier to evaluate than asking one page to represent both markets at once.
Audience: Wedding photographers serving more than one region who are deciding how to focus advertising and landing-page content while managing a limited initial scope.
Tension: A shared page may be convenient, but imagery and context that suit one region may not speak to couples considering a different setting.
Take: Treat geographic expansion and page design as linked choices: a business can start with the market it is prioritising and revisit the second market when it can represent that audience coherently.
Mechanism: In the reported discussion, the photographer said Colorado couples often want to see mountains while Florida couples often do not, and connected that difference to difficulty targeting both through one landing page. Different visual expectations can change what makes the page feel relevant to a couple evaluating the location.
Reader decision: Check whether each target region has meaningfully different visual expectations or reasons to choose the business, then decide whether one page can represent them or whether to focus first on one market.
Supporting source content (Zoom Meeting - 1st June - 7th June)
Selected turns below are separated in the original dialogue unless their timestamps are adjacent.

\[00:18:25] Carmen Cruickshank: Awesome. And you just wanted to do Orlando area, nothing with Denver right now, correct?
\[00:18:29] Shannon Lee: I think that's the plan, because I feel like it's hard to target both markets with one landing page, because a lot of my Colorado weddings want to see mountains, and a lot of my Florida weddings don't.
\[00:18:41] Shannon Lee: So I think at some point I might, you know.
\[00:18:46] Shannon Lee: try and target Colorado, but for now, we're just gonna go with Orlando.

Claim boundary: The regional preferences reflect this photographer's clients. Colorado targeting was a future possibility. No comparison of landing-page performance was reported.

**Original feedback:**

It's 1's across the board for me at this point.

The AI is identifying what we talked about on our calls, but it's missing what those conversations demonstrate about our expertise, our process, and the value we provide to our clients.

**Revised content or direction:**

Angle 1 | How we help wedding pros make their landing pages relevant to the couples they actually want to book
Original proposed angle: Give distinct wedding markets distinct landing page context
Content pillar / type: WARM · Process / behind the scenes
Audience: Wedding pros investing in advertising, especially those serving different markets or client types
Why this matters: An attractive portfolio is not automatically an effective ads landing page. Couples need to see imagery and messaging that reflect the wedding experience they are looking for.
WIA’s expertise or process: We work with clients to decide which market or audience to prioritize, then align landing-page imagery, messaging and campaign targeting with that audience. The client’s knowledge of their market shapes the strategy.
Evidence in the referenced call: Carmen confirmed the immediate geographic focus; Shannon explained that Colorado and Florida couples responded to different imagery, and chose Orlando first.
Editorial guardrail / next step: Show how landing page decisions are made collaboratively—not a generic rule that every region needs a separate page. No performance comparison was reported.

**Acceptance:** Preferred strategic direction, not final publication wording. Whitney said she added the aligned alternative tab in her October 12 Slack reply; the tab explicitly says proposed directions, not final hooks, scripts or proven-performance claims.

**Principles illustrated:** P033, P034, P035, P036, P044, P045, P106, P107.

**Limits:** The same general rejection applies to all six. No filled individual rating comments were recovered. The vendor’s thread identifies the batch as June 1–7, 2026; the individual calls’ dates and identities were not independently verified. Selected transcript turns are not complete recordings.

### D002 · Angle 2: Position candid work without promising no guidance → How we turn a wedding pro’s differentiators into messaging that accurately communicates the client experience

**Source/date:** [Open source](https://docs.google.com/document/d/1_LRWdO-rQ2ez9J0IWffrx9gt0L6j2nNlmH01iHxYuag/edit?usp=sharing); 2026-10-12. **Recovery status:** verified\_exchange.

**Original proposed content:**

Angle 2: Position candid work without promising no guidance
Argument: A wedding photographer's positioning is more useful when it distinguishes candid coverage from staged imagery without implying that couples will be left unguided when they want help.
Audience: Wedding photographers with an established visual style who are refining ad messaging for couples deciding whether the photographer's approach will suit them.
Tension: A strong shorthand such as “no posing” can signal a candid style, but may also suggest the photographer will not help with portraits when couples want direction.
Take: Describe the experience couples can expect across the day, including both candid coverage and requested portrait direction, rather than making an absolute claim that misstates the service.
Mechanism: The photographer reported that many couples value real moments and also ask for help with couple portraits. Messaging that reflects both parts can address the style preference without obscuring the practical support those couples may want.
Reader decision: Review prominent ad and landing-page claims for absolutes, then check whether they accurately describe both candid coverage and the guidance available for portraits.
Supporting source content (Zoom Meeting - 1st June - 7th June)

Selected turns below are separated in the original dialogue unless their timestamps are adjacent.

\[00:02:00] Shannon Lee: Um, the only thing I'd say is I, I do pose people, I just don't pose them awkwardly or stiff, so I don't know if I want one that says specifically no posing at all. I do take, like, 90% candids, and that is the branding that I base off of.
\[00:02:16] Shannon Lee: I just don't want someone to think that I'm never going to help them out when they're struggling.
\[00:03:45] Shannon Lee: most of my clients are like, yes, we want, like, all those real moments, we don't want you to pose us until we get to our couple's portraits, then can you please… can you please tell us what to do with our hands? And that's why I don't want them to think that I won't pose them at all, because a lot of them come to me, like, on consult calls.
\[00:04:42] Shannon Lee: Oh, what about authentic, not staged?

Claim boundary: This is a service and messaging distinction from the photographer's account. It does not demonstrate a change in inquiries or bookings. The quoted candid proportion is self-reported.

**Original feedback:**

It's 1's across the board for me at this point.

The AI is identifying what we talked about on our calls, but it's missing what those conversations demonstrate about our expertise, our process, and the value we provide to our clients.

**Revised content or direction:**

Angle 2 | How we turn a wedding pro’s differentiators into messaging that accurately communicates the client experience
Original proposed angle: Position candid work without promising no guidance
Content pillar / type: WARM · UVP / messaging process
Audience: Photographers and other wedding pros whose marketing language oversimplifies what makes them different
Why this matters: A differentiator can be true in spirit but misleading when expressed too absolutely. “No posing” risks promising an experience the photographer does not actually deliver.
WIA’s expertise or process: Our work includes pressure-testing ad and landing-page language with the client so the message reflects both their style and what couples actually value. The goal is compelling differentiation without sacrificing accuracy.
Evidence in the referenced call: Shannon clarified that most of her coverage is candid but couples still want guidance during portraits; she suggested “authentic, not staged.”
Editorial guardrail / next step: Use this as an example of collaborative messaging refinement. Do not imply WIA independently originated the revised phrase or that a change improved conversions.

**Acceptance:** Preferred strategic direction, not final publication wording. Whitney said she added the aligned alternative tab in her October 12 Slack reply; the tab explicitly says proposed directions, not final hooks, scripts or proven-performance claims.

**Principles illustrated:** P033, P034, P035, P036, P044, P045, P106, P107.

### D003 · Angle 3: Check buyer meaning before excluding a style keyword → What our keyword research process looks like—and why client conversations are part of it

**Source/date:** [Open source](https://docs.google.com/document/d/1_LRWdO-rQ2ez9J0IWffrx9gt0L6j2nNlmH01iHxYuag/edit?usp=sharing); 2026-10-12. **Recovery status:** verified\_exchange.

**Original proposed content:**

Angle 3: Check buyer meaning before excluding a style keyword
Argument: A search term that appears stylistically misaligned is not automatically a negative keyword when couples may use style labels imprecisely and some inquiries using the term may still be suitable.
Audience: Wedding photographers managing search campaigns who have a defined visual style and are weighing lead relevance against excluding potentially useful searches.
Tension: A term such as “light and airy” may seem inconsistent with a photographer's work, yet the photographer does not know whether every couple using it means the same thing.
Take: Treat a suspected mismatch as a question to investigate with actual search and inquiry evidence, rather than deciding from the label alone.
Mechanism: The photographer reported that some inquiries included the term and that one of roughly four or five people who mentioned it in the last year became a client. That small account does not establish keyword performance, but it illustrates why the search phrase, inquiry context, and eventual fit need to be considered before excluding it.
Reader decision: Review search-term records and the resulting inquiry and booking context for the term; decide whether the evidence shows a recurring mismatch before adding it as a negative keyword.
Supporting source content (Zoom Meeting - 1st June - 7th June)

Selected turns below are separated in the original dialogue unless their timestamps are adjacent.



\[00:29:33] Shannon Lee: light and airy is, like, a term that people use and, like, search for when it comes to photography, and that is not what I am, and I sometimes get inquiries who will put that in the contact form.
\[00:29:48] Shannon Lee: saying that's what they want. But I also think that I don't trust couples to actually know what they want or know how to properly describe what they're looking for. So I don't know if I would negative that out. Um, but that's the only other thing that I could kind of think of when it's clearly a misalign, a misalignment of a client.
\[00:30:05] Carmen Cruickshank: Mm-hmm. Normally, do those people that mention light in the area, do they end up becoming clients, or no?
\[00:30:12] Shannon Lee: I mean, I've had a couple in the last year mention it and I think one out of like four or five became my client.
\[00:30:19] Carmen Cruickshank: Okay, that's good. Yeah, we could definitely leave it there. And if we see a lot of people are searching it and it's just becoming irrelevant for some reason, then we can take it out. But that's good to know.
Claim boundary: The small, approximate client count is not an ad-attributed conversion rate. The recommendation is to investigate fit, not automatically retain or exclude the term.

**Original feedback:**

It's 1's across the board for me at this point.

The AI is identifying what we talked about on our calls, but it's missing what those conversations demonstrate about our expertise, our process, and the value we provide to our clients.

**Revised content or direction:**

Angle 3 | What our keyword research process looks like—and why client conversations are part of it
Original proposed angle: Check buyer meaning before excluding a style keyword
Content pillar / type: WARM · Process / education; possible CONVERT · Solution selling
Audience: Wedding businesses considering Google Ads or wondering what an agency actually does to manage them
Why this matters: A keyword can look wrong on paper while the real-world meaning and commercial value of the search are more nuanced. Decisions made without client context can discard potentially useful demand.
WIA’s expertise or process: We review search terms and negative-keyword candidates with clients, ask how those terms show up in real inquiries, and use that context alongside account data to make and revisit decisions. Technical expertise and client expertise both matter.
Evidence in the referenced call: Carmen asked whether people who used “light and airy” became clients. Shannon recalled approximately one booking among four or five such inquiries; Carmen recommended keeping the term under review.
Editorial guardrail / next step: Highlight the decision-making process, not a universal recommendation to retain “light and airy.” The anecdote is not an ad-attributed conversion rate.

**Acceptance:** Preferred strategic direction, not final publication wording. Whitney said she added the aligned alternative tab in her October 12 Slack reply; the tab explicitly says proposed directions, not final hooks, scripts or proven-performance claims.

### D004 · Angle 4: Make an all inclusive venue comparison about scope not just fees → How we help wedding venues position their value when their offering is different from competitors

**Source/date:** [Open source](https://docs.google.com/document/d/1_LRWdO-rQ2ez9J0IWffrx9gt0L6j2nNlmH01iHxYuag/edit?usp=sharing); 2026-10-12. **Recovery status:** verified\_exchange.

**Original proposed content:**

Angle 4: Make an all inclusive venue comparison about scope not just fees
Argument: When couples compare an all-inclusive venue with venue-only alternatives, showing what each option includes and what couples would need to source separately can make the comparison more meaningful than headline rental fees alone.
Audience: Owners of all-inclusive wedding venues whose prospective couples compare their package with local venue-only options and ask what the total wedding will involve or cost.
Tension: Couples may see a venue-only rental figure beside a larger all-inclusive price without yet knowing the services, items, and planning responsibilities represented by either option.
Take: Help couples compare the scope and responsibilities of the options before treating the visible venue fee as a like-for-like price comparison.
Mechanism: The venue owner said couples ask what is included and what the wedding will cost. She described using a comparison chart and estimated outside costs in tour follow-up; making comparable scope visible can help buyers evaluate what they would otherwise need to arrange, while the account does not establish a tested effect on bookings.
Reader decision: Inspect the comparison material and follow-up: does it clearly show what the package includes and distinguish that from estimated costs and tasks couples may take on elsewhere?
Supporting source content (Zoom Meeting - 1st June - 7th June)

Selected turns below are separated in the original dialogue unless their timestamps are adjacent.

So, they might see a venue rental cost of $10,000, and then they're looking at our cost, which is $30, and they clearly know there's more in there, but they don't really know the value of those things.

I do think, like, visually it could look better than it does, it's just kind of basic looking, um, but I have… I do think that that's helpful, and I try to kind of emphasize that with people in follow-up. When I'm doing my follow-ups after tours, or even if they've got a lot of questions ahead of time, yeah, like, look at everything we have, and then look at, like, what you're gonna.



\[00:33:10] Lauren Christensen: do on your own and, like, here's an estimated cost for what that will cost you to do that outside of this.



\[00:33:31] Whitney Hodges: So I do think on the landing page that that would be pretty powerful to include.

Original proposed angle: Make an all-inclusive venue comparison about scope, not just fees
Content pillar / type: WARM · Positioning / process; CONVERT · Expertise in action
Audience: Wedding venues—particularly those with a distinctive model, package or price point
Why this matters: Couples may compare two venues using headline prices even when the experiences, inclusions and responsibilities are fundamentally different. The venue’s marketing has to make that difference understandable.
WIA’s expertise or process: We identify what makes a venue’s offering different and look for ways to communicate that value throughout the inquiry journey—including ads, landing pages and follow-up materials. This is positioning work embedded in advertising strategy.

### D005 · Angle 5: Match wedding venue ad creative to the buyers next question → What goes into our wedding venue advertising strategy beyond creating attractive ads

**Source/date:** [Open source](https://docs.google.com/document/d/1_LRWdO-rQ2ez9J0IWffrx9gt0L6j2nNlmH01iHxYuag/edit?usp=sharing); 2026-10-12. **Recovery status:** verified\_exchange.

**Original proposed content:**

Angle 5: Match wedding venue ad creative to the buyers next question
Argument: A wedding venue's ad creative can serve different purposes for people at different stages: introduce the venue to unfamiliar audiences, invite an inquiry or tour when seeking action, and address choice questions for people already familiar with it.
Audience: Wedding venues planning or reviewing paid campaigns for audiences ranging from people who have not encountered the venue to people who have already inquired or scheduled a tour.
Tension: One creative approach may not simultaneously introduce an unfamiliar venue, prompt a direct response, and address the reasons a warm prospect might choose it over alternatives.
Take: Decide what the audience already knows and what decision the ad is meant to support before choosing its message and creative.
Mechanism: The proposed plan distinguishes cold-audience introductions, conversion ads asking for an inquiry or tour, and retargeting that addresses objections and reasons to book. The message changes with the audience's decision stage; the exchange describes an intended approach, not demonstrated campaign results.
Reader decision: For each audience group, identify its current relationship to the venue and the next buyer question or action the creative is intended to support.
Supporting source content (Zoom Meeting - 1st June - 7th June)

Selected turns below are separated in the original dialogue unless their timestamps are adjacent.

### D006 · Angle 6: Separate setup value from the recurring software commitment → What prospective clients find compelling about LeadLock—and how to surface that value in our messaging

**Source/date:** [Open source](https://docs.google.com/document/d/1_LRWdO-rQ2ez9J0IWffrx9gt0L6j2nNlmH01iHxYuag/edit?usp=sharing); 2026-10-12. **Recovery status:** verified\_exchange.

**Original proposed content:**

Angle 6: Separate setup value from the recurring software commitment
Argument: For a small wedding business considering lead-management software, an ongoing subscription can remain the decisive affordability concern even when the setup work seems worth paying for and the system appears useful.
Audience: Owners of small wedding businesses evaluating recurring software or lead-management costs, especially those with limited booking volume or lower-cost tools already in use.
Tension: A system can seem useful and its initial setup fee reasonable, while its continuing subscription feels difficult to justify against the business's scale and current alternatives.
Take: Assess the recurring commitment separately from implementation cost and feature appeal, using the business's actual scale and existing tools rather than treating interest as evidence of affordability.
Mechanism: In one sales conversation, a photographer who takes up to about 20 weddings a year distinguished the setup fee from the ongoing cost and compared it with a free tool she was using. Those differences help explain her reported decision not to proceed; they do not establish an affordability threshold for other businesses.
Reader decision: Before committing to a recurring platform, compare the continuing cost with the functions and workload it would replace or support, and assess that commitment against the business's own volume and budget.
Supporting source content (Zoom Meeting - 1st June - 7th June)

Selected turns below are separated in the original dialogue unless their timestamps are adjacent.
\[00:28:44] Zadie Ward: And I'm not… I'm quite a small business of… I only take, sort of, like, 20 weddings a year maximum, so I just don't think I'm ever gonna get that much money that that's gonna be viable.
\[00:28:54] Zadie Ward: Um, the setup fee doesn't bother me so much, because like you say, it's so much work, but I think it's the ongoing cost.
\[00:29:01] Zadie Ward: That might be a bit of an issue for me. Yeah, just being completely honest, because I wouldn't want to waste any more of your time, really.
\[00:29:11] Zadie Ward: But I'm gutted because it's an amazing, amazing system. And yeah, and I'm and I, you know, if anyone else I know in the industry is looking for something like that, I'll be sure to recommend it because like I say, it's the first time I've known something to do all of those things. So yeah, I think it's amazing, but it just might be a bit out of my reach.
\[00:29:44] Zadie Ward: Yeah, I think so, because mainly, like, I don't have enough people on there at the moment, so I'm using the free version. So again, it's just because I'm such a small company, I think. Whereas, I guess, for, like, a planner, or someone, you know, a venue, or someone who's got a lot more income coming in.
Claim boundary: This is one prospect's stated affordability concern. It does not establish a universal price threshold or a confirmed purchase outcome. Historical offer details are not current pricing.

**Original feedback:**

It's 1's across the board for me at this point.

The AI is identifying what we talked about on our calls, but it's missing what those conversations demonstrate about our expertise, our process, and the value we provide to our clients.

**Revised content or direction:**

Angle 6 | What prospective clients find compelling about LeadLock—and how to surface that value in our messaging
Original proposed angle: Separate setup value from the recurring software commitment
Content pillar / type: CONVERT · Voice of customer / solution selling; internal sales intelligence
Audience: Wedding pros who need a more connected inquiry-management and follow-up process
Why this matters: A prospect can recognize meaningful product value while still deciding that the ongoing cost does not fit their business. Their praise and their objection are two distinct insights.
WIA’s expertise or process: Analyze the full demo to identify the specific capabilities that impressed Zadie, the gaps in her current setup, and the outcomes she associated with LeadLock. Translate verified themes into product messaging or demonstrations; separately retain the recurring-price objection for sales learning.
Evidence in the referenced call: Zadie called the system “amazing” and said it was the first time she had seen something “do all of those things,” while explaining that the monthly cost was difficult for her business to justify.
Editorial guardrail / next step: IMPORTANT: The supplied excerpt does not identify which features impressed her. Review earlier portions of the same call before naming benefits. Do not turn her affordability concern into a generic lesson encouraging prospects to question the subscription.

**Acceptance:** Preferred strategic direction, not final publication wording. Whitney said she added the aligned alternative tab in her October 12 Slack reply; the tab explicitly says proposed directions, not final hooks, scripts or proven-performance claims.

**Principles illustrated:** P033, P034, P035, P036, P044, P045, P106, P107.

**Limits:** The same general rejection applies to all six. No filled individual rating comments were recovered. The vendor’s thread identifies the batch as June 1–7, 2026; the individual calls’ dates and identities were not independently verified. Selected transcript turns are not complete recordings.

## Part E — Content Atelier implementation rules

These are **proposed rules**, not installed application settings. A rule supported only by memory is held for original-source recovery or explicit confirmation. Inferred patterns remain soft unless the user later strengthens them. Generated handbook scoring anchors and its at-least-4 threshold are recommendations for calibration, not recovered approved requirements.

### Gate 1 · Angle selection

Approve the central point, intended prospect, primary strategic purpose, why it is worth content now, what WIA actually contributed and the evidence boundaries. A correct client topic can still be the wrong public angle. Reject unsupported attribution/results before ranking. The proposed SELECT/REFRAME/HOLD/INTERNAL/SKIP choices can make the judgment explicit; their names are implementation recommendations.

### Gate 2 · Brief review

Approve audience, narrative/message spine, source claim map, appropriate depth, offer fit, transformation and CTA before producing finished pieces. Keep source participant separate from content audience. Label new illustrative examples as illustrations. A preferred angle with missing evidence can remain on hold.

### Gate 3 · Draft review

Approve the exact copy and asset versions. Check speaker/client voice, claim accuracy, current exclusions, redundancy, caption/reel/cover fit, readable visual timing and dated offer facts. Track partial approval separately. A changed angle or claim should cause dependent work to be reviewed again; a thank-you, a scheduled date or a generated file is not approval.

### Rule register

Hard constraints chiefly protect meaning, scope, attribution and approval. Strong preferences shape editorial choice. Soft guidelines preserve context and are not automatic blockers. Each rule’s evidence is the matching P record’s cited E records.

|Rule / evidence|Do|Avoid|When|Level / gates|Activation|
|-|-|-|-|-|-|
|R001 / P001 / E001, E002|Explain the buyer and positioning work that informs the advertising decision.|Reducing WIA expertise to knowing which buttons to click.|global\_editorial|strong\_preference / Angle, Brief, Draft|proposed\_ready\_for\_review|
|R002 / P002 / E003, E004|Connect attention to relevance, trust, inquiry and booking at the scope the evidence supports.|Treating views or cheap clicks as success by themselves.|global\_editorial|strong\_preference / Angle, Brief, Draft|proposed\_ready\_for\_review|
|R003 / P003 / E005, E006|Name the relevant foundation and show how paid visibility works with it.|Promising that buying traffic repairs every business problem.|global\_editorial|strong\_preference / Angle, Brief, Draft|proposed\_ready\_for\_review|
|R004 / P004 / E007|Explain complementary roles and the particular business context.|Saying ads replace branding, content, reputation or all organic work.|global\_editorial|strong\_preference / Angle, Brief, Draft|proposed\_ready\_for\_review|
|R005 / P005 / E008, E009|Show the specific continuing work and what manual burden it reduces.|Treating inconsistent posting as a character flaw or promising no owner involvement.|global\_editorial|strong\_preference / Angle, Brief, Draft|proposed\_ready\_for\_review|
|R006 / P006 / E010|Include supported practical and emotional consequences of the workflow.|Making the transformation only a revenue number.|automation\_offer\_and\_system\_stories|strong\_preference / Angle, Brief, Draft|proposed\_ready\_for\_review|
|R007 / P007 / E011, E012|Distinguish experience feedback from research into criteria, objections and competing alternatives.|Calling a newly signed client’s market-research form a completed-service experience survey.|global\_editorial|strong\_preference / Angle, Brief, Draft|proposed\_ready\_for\_review|
|R008 / P008 / E013, E014|Keep original quotes separate from interpretation and final brand copy.|Fabricating, blending or presenting AI rewrites as customer quotes.|global\_editorial|hard\_constraint / Angle, Brief, Draft|proposed\_ready\_for\_review|
|R009 / P009 / E015|Make useful client questions and provisional decisions visible.|Presenting WIA as ignoring the client or taking credit for client insights.|global\_editorial|strong\_preference / Angle, Brief, Draft|proposed\_ready\_for\_review|
|R010 / P010 / E016, E017|Explain the specific audience relationship, message objective and next step.|Assuming one ad or a fixed funnel sequence fits every buyer.|campaign\_strategy\_content|strong\_preference / Angle, Brief, Draft|proposed\_ready\_for\_review|
|R011 / P011 / E018, E019|Name the metric, its role and the downstream evidence available.|Conflating a platform conversion with a qualified inquiry, booking or attributable revenue.|global\_editorial|hard\_constraint / Angle, Brief, Draft|proposed\_ready\_for\_review|
|R012 / P012 / E020, E021|Show what testing or accumulated information helps the team decide.|Promising a single launch or a trend removes the need to learn.|global\_editorial|strong\_preference / Angle, Brief, Draft|proposed\_ready\_for\_review|
|R013 / P013 / E022|State the evidence and tradeoff behind a spend recommendation.|Using spend more as the explanation for every weak result.|budget\_and\_optimization\_content|strong\_preference / Angle, Brief, Draft|proposed\_ready\_for\_review|
|R014 / P014 / E023|Use results as dated, attributed evidence with context and limitations.|Presenting another client’s counts as a prospect forecast.|global\_editorial|hard\_constraint / Angle, Brief, Draft|proposed\_ready\_for\_review|

### Evidence and approval behavior proposed for the application

Keep a dated source record, exact quote, speaker, source location, context and evidence kind with each claim. Separate what the participant said, what was implemented, what was measured and the analyst’s interpretation. Keep approval actor, timestamp, exact version and scope as separate fields. Preserve old records when a new instruction supersedes them. Do not silently promote a remembered statement or repeated generated synthesis into a verified user rule.

For before/after results, require the unit, baseline, timeframe, approximation and attribution. For offer details, require a dated business record. For buyer quotes, preserve the original words. For a demo reaction, retrieve the feature moment before assigning a feature endorsement. For uncertain transcripts, verify the material phrase or number rather than making every public sentence hedge.

A useful learning loop records the rejected direction, the user’s reason, the approved scope and the actual change. A new reference endorsement can update context; it should not overwrite a specific prior exclusion automatically.

## Part F — Conflicts and gaps

Contradictions are retained below. The resolutions are interpretations proposed for the system, not claims that Whitney has individually approved every resolution.

### C001 · Old hype and contrast copy versus later voice restrictions

**Status:** CONFLICTING.

**Evidence relationship:** 2024 email says secret sauce, spilling the tea and dynamite. January/March 2025 explicitly approved captions use game-changer, em dashes, urgency and contrasts. The endorsed 2026 guide discourages hype words. Later memory/retrieval reports specific exclusions.

**Proposed resolution:** Preserve historical examples; generate new WIA copy under the latest specific exclusions and current voice reference. Do not rewrite the evidence to make history look consistent.

**Pointers:** P054, P055, P056, P057, P085; [S018](#s018), [S013](#s013), [S014](#s014), [S005](#s005), [S032](#s032), [S033](#s033).

**Confirmation needed for implementation/public reuse:** Confirm the exact scope of the It’s not X/It’s Y and em-dash exclusions if the system needs automated hard-block behavior rather than a default review preference.

### C002 · A universal confidence slogan versus practical information needs

**Status:** CONTEXT-DEPENDENT.

**Evidence relationship:** Founder guide says buyers do not need more information but need confidence. The knowledge-base funnel email says prospects wanted more information before a call; scope/pricing correspondence provides necessary detail.

**Proposed resolution:** Treat the slogan as a strategic emphasis on decision confidence, not permission to withhold needed information.

**Pointers:** P015, P022, P099; [S005](#s005), [S008](#s008), [S010](#s010).

### C003 · Calm founder voice versus polarizing coach prompts

**Status:** CONTEXT-DEPENDENT.

**Evidence relationship:** Savannah’s endorsed strategy contains forceful suggestions, profanity, sweeping platform/agency claims and contrast hooks. The founder guide emphasizes curiosity and respectful challenge.

**Proposed resolution:** Retain the opinion and ATTRACT purpose while checking the actual claim, current corrections and Whitney’s voice. Strategy endorsement does not mean every suggested hook is approved verbatim.

**Pointers:** P016, P037, P039, P048, P054, P102; [S005](#s005), [S006](#s006).

### C004 · Educational pillar versus rejection of educational mini-lessons

**Status:** CONTEXT-DEPENDENT.

**Evidence relationship:** The original Content Atelier six angles were rejected as too narrow and academic; the actual strategy still includes education.

**Proposed resolution:** Keep worthwhile education, but select it for a relevant point and visible reasoning. Do not turn the feedback into a prohibition on teaching.

**Pointers:** P033, P036, P040; [S002](#s002), [S004](#s004), [S006](#s006).

### C005 · Need a foundation versus come back when you know your UVP

**Status:** CONTEXT-DEPENDENT.

**Evidence relationship:** Positioning precedes effective campaigns; WIA’s onboarding helps develop that positioning. The remembered rejected email lead implied prospects must solve it first.

**Proposed resolution:** Show the foundation-building work as part of the relevant offer and clarify actual readiness/fit.

**Pointers:** P001, P019, P093, P109; [S013](#s013), [S012](#s012), [S032](#s032).

### C006 · Traditional offer recommendation versus rare ELEVATE exceptions

**Status:** CONTEXT-DEPENDENT.

**Evidence relationship:** Memory says LeadLock required with rare exceptions. The September 29 sent email says ad management without it is technically possible but the outside backend would be the client’s responsibility.

**Proposed resolution:** Do not promise an exception or imply WIA builds outside workflows. Use the latest contract/offer record before publishing any standard-policy claim.

**Pointers:** P096, P098, P110; [S010](#s010), [S009](#s009), [S032](#s032).

**Confirmation needed for implementation/public reuse:** Current contractual LeadLock requirement and any exception policy need a dated business record before use as present-tense fact.

### C007 · No migration versus taking client-specific work with you

**Status:** CONTEXT-DEPENDENT.

**Evidence relationship:** Short memory descriptions stress non-migrating workflows. The sent email explicitly distinguishes contacts/data and client-specific copy, sequences, pipeline/form structure from functioning infrastructure.

**Proposed resolution:** Keep both sides. No infrastructure migration does not mean clients lose every asset. Portable structure/content does not mean a working system is migrated or rebuilt.

**Pointers:** P098; [S010](#s010), [S032](#s032).

### C008 · Established-client default versus beginner exceptions

**Status:** CONTEXT-DEPENDENT.

**Evidence relationship:** The worksheet says clients are usually established, and MAA historical source copy focuses on marketing-savvy owners in growth. The worksheet also records a successful brand-new planner exception.

**Proposed resolution:** Use stage as a default fit criterion, not a blanket rule that new businesses cannot benefit from advertising.

**Pointers:** P017, P019, P024, P029; [S007](#s007), [S008](#s008), [S025](#s025).

### C009 · Short carousels versus a 12-slide approved-for-design story

**Status:** CONTEXT-DEPENDENT.

**Evidence relationship:** Retrieved May feedback dislikes thin 13-slide carousels. August 13 direct Slack provides a developed 12-slide capacity story for design.

**Proposed resolution:** Use substantive progression and necessary length. The source does not establish a fixed 7-, 10- or 12-slide ceiling.

**Pointers:** P071, P075; [S013](#s013), [S033](#s033).

**Confirmation needed for implementation/public reuse:** A numeric slide maximum would require explicit confirmation; it should not be inferred.

### C010 · Short video overlays versus substantive carousel text

**Status:** CONTEXT-DEPENDENT.

**Evidence relationship:** Whitney praised visual reveals without big text chunks in a short reel. She also dislikes too little substance across many carousel slides.

**Proposed resolution:** Fit text density to the format. Video reading time and carousel argument development are different constraints.

**Pointers:** P071, P072, P073; [S013](#s013), [S015](#s015), [S033](#s033).

### C011 · Avoid stock b-roll versus a grid using stock photos

**Status:** CONTEXT-DEPENDENT.

**Evidence relationship:** February direction says avoid stock video if possible; March grid planning explicitly alternates brand and stock photos.

**Proposed resolution:** Keep media type and availability qualifier. There is no supported ban on all stock imagery.

**Pointers:** P074, P075; [S013](#s013).

### C012 · Exact visual directions versus willingness to test

**Status:** CONTEXT-DEPENDENT.

**Evidence relationship:** January 25 allows covers without text; February requests cover text for the grid. A cover crop correction allows a performance tradeoff. March accepts either reel color; another specific chart has exact #5271FF.

**Proposed resolution:** Retain asset and campaign context; do not turn one design choice into an immutable brand-wide rule.

**Pointers:** P075, P076; [S013](#s013), [S014](#s014).

### C013 · CTA defaults versus campaign-specific conversion invitations

**Status:** CONTEXT-DEPENDENT.

**Evidence relationship:** Savannah defaults Attract/Warm to engagement and Convert to DM/inquiry/call. Actual WIA educational captions sometimes use DM ADS; a February campaign shifted to link-in-bio due low DM responsiveness.

**Proposed resolution:** Use purpose as the default and the current approved campaign as the override. No universal DM keyword is established.

**Pointers:** P067, P068, P069, P114; [S006](#s006), [S013](#s013).

### C014 · Posting cadence changed over time

**Status:** CONFLICTING.

**Evidence relationship:** January 2025 stopped mirrored TikTok content and March retained two reels/two carousels weekly. The October-endorsed strategy recommends daily TikTok/Instagram patterns.

**Proposed resolution:** Keep the newer strategy as a reference baseline; get the current execution plan rather than assume it has been fully implemented. Trend share is approximately 5% of ATTRACT, not of the total calendar.

**Pointers:** P043, P118; [S006](#s006), [S013](#s013).

**Confirmation needed for implementation/public reuse:** Current platform priority and actual weekly cadence need confirmation if Content Atelier will create a calendar.

### C015 · Recurring ad strategy versus legitimate short promotions

**Status:** CONTEXT-DEPENDENT.

**Evidence relationship:** The sources advocate evergreen long-game advertising and also contain positive promotional case stories and annual giveaways.

**Proposed resolution:** Ongoing strategic advertising and purposeful limited promotions can coexist. Do not claim every short campaign is ineffective or every offer needs urgency.

**Pointers:** P012, P039, P077, P099; [S018](#s018), [S008](#s008), [S006](#s006), [S013](#s013).

### C016 · Direct attribution versus historical shorthand in case stories

**Status:** CONFLICTING.

**Evidence relationship:** One guide hook says 17 weddings to 90; the source-copy compilation generally says events. Historical revenue doubling, ROAS and lead counts lack underlying exports in this audit.

**Proposed resolution:** Keep exact units, timeframe, baseline, approximate language and attribution. Total business growth is not automatically ad-attributed growth. Treat historical claims as source examples pending result verification.

**Pointers:** P011, P014, P078, P097, P106; [S005](#s005), [S008](#s008), [S011](#s011), [S004](#s004).

**Confirmation needed for implementation/public reuse:** Underlying case-study data and precise booking/event units require verification before reuse.

### C017 · Marketing opinion versus universally true technical claim

**Status:** CONTEXT-DEPENDENT.

**Evidence relationship:** Coach prompt says stop boosting and contains broad platform/agency motive claims. Project playbooks use contextual account decisions and data.

**Proposed resolution:** Store opinions as WIA positioning, not scientifically proven or universal technical facts. Specific technical recommendations should retain account context and current primary evidence.

**Pointers:** P010, P013, P025, P102; [S006](#s006), [S019](#s019), [S020](#s020).

### C018 · Preferred alternative direction versus missing process evidence

**Status:** CONTEXT-DEPENDENT.

**Evidence relationship:** The review tab’s landing-page and LeadLock directions are preferred, yet selected snippets omit parts of WIA’s contribution or the features that impressed Zadie.

**Proposed resolution:** A preferred direction can remain HOLD. Retrieve the precise surrounding turns before claiming a full workflow, client endorsement or outcome.

**Pointers:** P036, P045, P106, P107; [S004](#s004), [S009](#s009).

### C019 · 2025 data-heavy copy versus current source integrity

**Status:** HISTORICAL.

**Evidence relationship:** February 2025 approved captions include 78%, 391%, 98%, 20%, 45% and 6% statistics without their external research sources in the retrieved thread. Later retrieved feedback objects to an unsupported 87% statistic.

**Proposed resolution:** Publication or approval proves an editorial decision occurred; it does not independently validate the statistic. Do not import these numbers as verified wedding-industry benchmarks.

**Pointers:** P008, P011, P014, P085; [S013](#s013), [S033](#s033).

**Confirmation needed for implementation/public reuse:** Original studies, definitions, populations and dates are needed for any statistical reuse.

### C020 · Older framework shorthand versus the current endorsed sequence

**Status:** HISTORICAL.

**Evidence relationship:** Retrieval summaries report Hook/Problem/Transformation/Solution/CTA shorthand in August and September. The actual endorsed strategy and Version 2.0 guide specify Hook/Problems-Desires-Symptoms/Main Point/Transformation/CTA.

**Proposed resolution:** Use the current documented sequence. A solution can appear where the main point and transformation naturally call for it; do not reorder the current framework solely from a remembered shorthand.

**Pointers:** P065, P066, P067; [S033](#s033), [S005](#s005), [S006](#s006).

### Missing evidence and subjects requiring confirmation

|Gap|Subject|What is missing|Consequence|
|-|-|-|-|
|GAP001|Original historical ChatGPT feedback|Full turns, original drafts, actual revised text, approval messages and direct pointers for the October 2 UVP/caption, August tracking email, July buyer-touchpoint cover and other memory-only findings.|Keep MEMORY-ONLY / SOURCE NOT VERIFIED labels; do not invent missing revisions.|
|GAP002|Source excerpts behind preferred angles|Surrounding Carmen/Shannon page-review turns, actual original call identifiers/dates, earlier Zadie demo and verified feature reactions.|Preferred direction may remain HOLD; a generic product feature list cannot fill a missing endorsement.|
|GAP003|Result verification|Ad spend/time windows, exact events-versus-weddings units, attribution, baseline and booked/revenue exports for historical 59 leads/9 bookings, 17-to-90, doubled revenue and other results.|Source-copy presence is not independent result validation.|
|GAP004|Current offer truth|A dated authoritative offer/contract record for prices, duration, deliverables, required LeadLock policy, exception scope, message fees, migration rights, availability and staff.|Use dated September correspondence as evidence of what was communicated then; do not certify today’s terms.|
|GAP005|Hard style exclusions|Exact current scope of em dashes, contrast constructions, struggling lead-ins, direct quotation exceptions and client-brand exceptions.|Use conservative WIA drafting defaults, preserve historic originals, and obtain confirmation before hard machine blockers with broad scope.|
|GAP006|Current content calendar and CTA plan|Which coached cadence is actually being executed, present platform priority, pillar mix beyond the approximately 5% ATTRACT trends note, active CTA keywords and current promotions.|Do not infer a calendar or reuse dated urgency from sample content.|
|GAP007|Current visual system|Approved 2026 palette, cover rules, font hierarchy, grid design and actual source visuals/video after edits.|Keep 2025 #5271FF and three-template patterns asset-specific/historical; exact screenshot/pixel execution not independently verified.|
|GAP008|Statistical research and customer quote approvals|Original studies for historic response/open-rate percentages, survey denominators, permissions and present validity of named public examples.|Do not publish percentages as verified wedding benchmarks or infer public naming permission from an internal recording.|
|GAP009|Application rule calibration|Explicit acceptance of handbook scoring thresholds, automatic rejection thresholds, rule severity and current enforcement behavior.|The proposed rules are reviewable implementation guidance, not deployed or approved application settings.|
|GAP010|Current standard bio and service-page wording|Latest approved public copy and dated team descriptors.|Use the verified team-leadership principle, not stale age, capacity or personnel claims.|

The audit proceeds with these gaps labeled. It does not require an answer to complete the delivered extraction. Confirmation becomes necessary when a missing fact is used as current public copy, when the application must enforce an ambiguous preference as a hard blocker, or when a missing version/approval is being represented as accepted.

## Part G — Machine-readable knowledge representation

The companion **Content\_Atelier\_Knowledge\_2026-10-14.json** is the complete export. It includes source coverage; 33 evidence-source records plus the 45-file inspection catalog; 189 exact-evidence records; 123 findings; 20 editorial decision cases; 116 indexed editorial-message occasions; 20 conflicts; 10 gaps; vocabulary/phrase references; and one proposed rule per finding.

```json
{
  "schema\_version": "1.0.0",
  "audit\_id": "WIA-CONTENT-ATELIER-2026-10-14",
  "scope\_complete": false,
  "collections": \[
    "coverage",
    "sources",
    "evidence",
    "findings",
    "decisions",
    "conflicts",
    "gaps",
    "lexicons",
    "implementation\_rules",
    "approval\_gate\_specification",
    "source\_integrity\_policy"
  ],
  "identity\_example": {
    "preference\_id": "P033",
    "evidence\_ids": \[
      "E049"
    ],
    "rule\_id": "R033",
    "gates": \[
      "angle\_selection",
      "brief\_review"
    ]
  },
  "null\_semantics": "Not recovered or unknown; never inferred rejection or approval"
}
```

For application use, join `findings.evidence\_ids` to `evidence.id`, then `evidence.source\_id` to `sources.id`. Rules retain `preference\_id`, `evidence\_ids`, constraint level, gate, status and activation. Decision cases retain separate original content, feedback, revision and acceptance. `scope\_complete` stays false because complete corpus coverage cannot be established. Stable IDs should be appended, not renumbered, when evidence is added.

### Integrity checks performed

All quoted fragments associated with retrieved text were checked for exact word/punctuation containment after whitespace normalization. All source, evidence, preference, rule and decision references were checked for valid IDs. Missing source URLs were left absent rather than manufactured. Memory-only findings, historical business amounts, incomplete before/after cases and approval limits remain explicit. No ad outcomes, live policy freshness or visual/audio accuracy were inferred from file presence.

The deliverables contain extracted evidence and implementation proposals. No new marketing content was drafted, no external communications were sent and no application rules were deployed.
